Safelight is built on RAWmakase and on the open-source software below.

========================================================================
RawNIND UtNet2 (Bayer), converted for the Neural Engine
------------------------------------------------------------------------
AI Denoise uses RawNIND by Benoit Brummer (UCLouvain), model UtNet2 (Bayer), licensed under CC BY 4.0 (https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/). Source: https://github.com/trougnouf/rawnind_jddc. Paper: Benoit Brummer and Christophe De Vleeschouwer, Joint Denoising, Demosaicing, and Compression: A Recommended Paradigm for Learning-Based Image Workflows (EUVIP 2026); preprint Learning Joint Denoising, Demosaicing, and Compression from the Raw Natural Image Noise Dataset, arXiv:2501.08924 (https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.08924). Trained on the RawNIND dataset (https://doi.org/10.14428/DVN/DEQCIM).

Changes: RAWmakase converted the original PyTorch checkpoint to ONNX (model_bayer.onnx, SHA-256 5111f19a66a9377002e2ff4ca87de8e3303085b33e6dc11746e42ce811bf6cc1), the weights unchanged. Safelight converted that to a half-precision Core ML model for the Neural Engine (RawNIND-ANE.mlpackage): each convolution's weights and bias multiplied by powers of two so its values fit half precision, the weights then rounded to half precision. scripts/convert-denoise-ane.py in Safelight's repository (https://github.com/ljbergmann/safelight) makes it. Provided as is, without warranties. Safelight is not endorsed by the authors.

UperNet ConvNeXt-base (Select Sky), converted for the Neural Engine
------------------------------------------------------------------------
Select Sky uses UperNet (Xiao et al., 2018) with ConvNeXt (Liu et al., Meta AI, 2022), trained on ADE20K (Zhou et al., MIT CSAIL) by OpenMMLab: openmmlab/upernet-convnext-base, licensed under the MIT licence according to its model card. The checkpoint was trained on ADE20K, whose images MIT offers for non-commercial research and education.

Changes: RAWmakase exported the checkpoint to ONNX (upernet-convnext-base-sky-fp16.onnx, SHA-256 ed75b2ad81ad209949c9580071ed486a72a423037822a49faa4e98991a43b891), its weights in half precision and only the sky's probability kept. Safelight converted that to a half-precision Core ML model for the Neural Engine (Sky-ANE.mlpackage), one function per photo shape. scripts/convert-sky-ane.py in Safelight's repository (https://github.com/ljbergmann/safelight) makes it. Provided as is, without warranties.

MIT License

SAM 2.1 small's image encoder and DETR (Select Subject), converted for Core ML
------------------------------------------------------------------------
Select Subject and Select Background use Segment Anything Model 2 (SAM 2), Ravi et al., Meta AI, 2024, https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam2 (facebook/sam2.1-hiera-small), and End-to-End Object Detection with Transformers (DETR), Carion et al., Facebook AI Research, 2020, https://github.com/facebookresearch/detr (facebook/detr-resnet-50-panoptic, trained on COCO), both licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0, whose text follows.

Changes: the Hugging Face onnx-community exported SAM 2.1 small to ONNX, and Xenova exported DETR (its weights in half precision). Safelight converted SAM 2's image encoder and DETR from those files to a Core ML model run on the GPU (Subject-CoreML.mlpackage): the encoder in half precision, DETR in single precision with one function per photo shape. scripts/convert-subject-coreml.py in Safelight's repository (https://github.com/ljbergmann/safelight) makes it. Provided as is, without warranties.

                              Apache License
                        Version 2.0, January 2004
                     http://www.apache.org/licenses/
